Hi, thanks. Yeah, so... honestly, what finally made me come in is that I keep getting up during our long sales strategy meetings at work. I know I’m supposed to stay seated, but I just... I end up getting up to refill my water, or I'll just go pace by the printer. Which is actually kind of funny because our office printer is practically an antique, it jams every single time we try to print on glossy paper for the pitch decks. My client last week—this big regional car dealership—wanted physical brochures for some reason, and I spent twenty minutes just fighting with that machine, getting ink on my good blouse. But anyway, yeah, even if I don't actually need to print anything at all, I'll just find myself walking over there or getting more water when everyone else is just sitting down listening to the Q3 projections.

Anxious or on edge? Um... not really? I mean, I don't think so. I actually really like my weekends. Oh, except for Sundays, sometimes I get that weird Sunday evening feeling, you know? Repeating thoughts? Um... like, getting stuck on something? No, I don't really get that. I mean, I get songs stuck in my head all the time, obviously. Urges to do something over and over? Um... no, not really. I mean, I don't feel like I *have* to do anything like that. Well, I do constantly check my phone, but everyone does that, right? Especially in my job. If you miss an email from a client, it's like the end of the world. Just last week, this one client—he's the VP of marketing for this organic skincare line—he emailed me on a Saturday morning asking for a complete revision of the deck we presented on Thursday. A Saturday! And of course, I didn't see it until Sunday afternoon because I was at my friend Sarah's wedding. It was upstate, in this beautiful converted barn. The lighting was incredible, all these fairy lights strung up across the rafters. But the food was... honestly, kind of a letdown. They had this vegan station that was just sad, wilted kale and some questionable tofu. I mean, I get wanting to be inclusive, but at least make it taste good, you know? My boyfriend ended up ordering a pizza to the hotel room afterward. But yeah, if I hadn't checked my phone when we got back, Monday morning would have been a complete disaster. So I guess I check my email a lot, but it's not like a weird urge, it's just work. So no, nothing like what you're asking about.

Just last week, this one client—he's the VP of marketing for this organic skincare line—he emailed me on a Saturday morning asking for a complete revision of the deck we presented on Thursday. A Saturday! And of course, I didn't see it until Sunday afternoon because I was at my friend Sarah's wedding. It was upstate, in this beautiful converted barn. The lighting was incredible, all these fairy lights strung up across the rafters. But the food was... honestly, kind of a letdown. They had this vegan station that was just sad, wilted kale and some questionable tofu. I mean, I get wanting to be inclusive, but at least make it taste good, you know? Avoid activities? Like, because I'm worried about getting stuck on them? Um... no, I don't think I do that. I mean, I avoid things all the time, but mostly just because I don't want to do them. Like laundry. I will literally buy new underwear rather than do laundry if I'm having a busy week. It's terrible, I know. My closet is basically a graveyard of clothes with tags still on them because I keep buying stuff for events instead of just washing what I have. I bought this amazing emerald green silk blouse for a pitch meeting last month, and I haven't worn it since because silk is just impossible to clean. You have to take it to the dry cleaner, and the one on my corner closed down. Now it's a bubble tea place. Which is great, honestly, because their taro milk tea is incredible, but it really doesn't help my laundry situation. The guy who runs it used to work in finance and just quit to open a boba shop. I kind of admire that, you know? Just dropping everything to do something completely different. I think about doing that sometimes, just quitting advertising and opening a dog rescue or something. But then I remember I have rent to pay. So... yeah, I don't avoid things because of having to do them a certain way. Just normal avoiding stuff.

Fidgeting? Um... no, not really. I mean, I guess I shift around a bit if I'm sitting for a long time, but nothing crazy. Like when we go to the movies. My boyfriend loves those really long, slow indie films. We went to see this three-hour black-and-white French film last weekend, and the seats at that theater are just awful. They have those old velvet ones that look cool but offer zero back support. I swear my lower back was screaming by the second act. And the guy next to us kept crinkling a candy wrapper. It's like, who eats hard candies at a movie theater anymore? Just get popcorn like a normal person. Popcorn is basically the only reason I even agree to go to the movies anyway. They use real butter at this place, which is amazing, but it gets all over your hands. I always have to bring extra napkins because I hate that greasy feeling on my fingers. It reminds me of this hand cream my mom used to use when I was a kid. It smelled like lavender and old books, and she would always try to put it on me in the winter when my hands got dry. I hated it. But anyway, no, I don't think I'm unusually restless or anything. Just normal fidgeting when I'm uncomfortable.

I swear my lower back was screaming by the second act. And the guy next to us kept crinkling a candy wrapper. It's like, who eats hard candies at a movie theater anymore? Just get popcorn like a normal person. Popcorn is basically the only reason I even agree to go to the movies anyway. They use real butter at this place, which is amazing, but it gets all over your hands. I always have to bring extra napkins because I hate that greasy feeling on my fingers. It reminds me of this hand cream my mom used to use when I was a kid. It smelled like lavender and old books, and she would always try to put it on me in the winter when my hands got dry. I hated it. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Focus on things outside of work? Wait, outside of work? Actually, it happens at work too. Like, a lot. Especially in those longer strategy meetings or when I have to read through a massive client brief. I always start out super focused, you know, pen in hand, ready to take notes. But then after a few minutes, I'll realize I've just been staring at the exact same slide or the same paragraph for who knows how long, and I have absolutely no idea what the last point was. It's so frustrating. It happened just yesterday when we were reviewing the Q3 projections for this new beverage client. They're doing this line of sparkling waters infused with adaptogens. Have you tried those? They're everywhere now. Ashwagandha, maca root, all that stuff. I tried the grapefruit rosemary one, and honestly, it just tasted like dirt. Fancy, carbonated dirt. They charge like four dollars a can for it, too. It's wild what people will pay for if you put it in a sleek pastel can and tell them it'll reduce their stress. I mean, my stress would be reduced if I didn't have to sit through a two-hour meeting about carbonated dirt, but here we are. My coworker, Ben, he drinks like three of them a day. I don't know how his stomach handles it. Anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the focus thing. Yeah, I just drift off and totally lose my place.

My mom used to call it the \"Sunday scaries.\" She would always make a big roast dinner on Sundays to try and make it feel better, with those little roasted potatoes that get crispy on the outside? I tried to make them last week, but I think my oven runs hot because they got completely charred on the bottom. It’s a gas oven, which I thought I wanted, but honestly, it’s been a nightmare to clean. I spent an hour scrubbing the grates with baking soda. But anyway... no, I wouldn't say I feel anxious or on edge normally. Just the normal amount, I guess?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions or rituals outside of work, like checking things or arranging items in a specific way?\n\nPatient: Rituals? Like checking things? Um... no, I don't think so. I don't really have any rituals. Unless you count my morning coffee, which is honestly the only way I survive the commute. I have to get it from this specific little cart near my apartment because the big chain places always burn the espresso. The guy who runs the cart, Hector, he knows my order by heart now. It's a lifesaver because the L train has been completely unpredictable lately. Last Tuesday, I was stuck underground for forty-five minutes with no cell service. Just staring at this ad for a dermatology clinic that was peeling off the wall. It's wild how much money they spend on subway ads when half of them are just vandalized within a week anyway. I was actually thinking about pitching a digital out-of-home campaign to one of my clients instead, it's way cleaner. But, um... wait. What did you ask me? Something about checking things? Yeah, no, I don't really do anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and are difficult to stop?\n\nPatient: Repeating thoughts? Um... like, getting stuck on something? No, I don't really get that. I mean, I get songs stuck in my head all the time, obviously. Like right now, I've had that one pop song from the car commercial playing on loop since yesterday morning. It's actually a really smart campaign, they licensed an indie track and now it's everywhere. We were talking about doing something similar for this boutique fitness brand I'm working with, but their budget is just ridiculously tight. They want champagne results on a tap water budget, you know? And the client lead keeps emailing me at like, 10 PM with these \"urgent\" ideas that are basically just things I already pitched them three weeks ago. I finally had to put my phone on Do Not Disturb after 9, which my boyfriend was thrilled about because my phone screen keeps waking him up. He's such a light sleeper. But anyway... no, I don't have any, like, repeating thoughts that I can't stop. Just normal stuff like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a strong urge to perform a specific behavior or task over and over?\n\nPatient: Urges to do something over and over? Um... no, not really. If you miss an email from a client, it's like the end of the world. Just last week, this one client—he's the VP of marketing for this organic skincare line—he emailed me on a Saturday morning asking for a complete revision of the deck we presented on Thursday. A Saturday! And of course, I didn't see it until Sunday afternoon because I was at my friend Sarah's wedding. It was upstate, in this beautiful converted barn. The lighting was incredible, all these fairy lights strung up across the rafters. But the food was... honestly, kind of a letdown. They had this vegan station that was just sad, wilted kale and some questionable tofu. I mean, I get wanting to be inclusive, but at least make it taste good, you know? My boyfriend ended up ordering a pizza to the hotel room afterward. But yeah, if I hadn't checked my phone when we got back, Monday morning would have been a complete disaster. So I guess I check my email a lot, but it's not like a weird urge, it's just work. So no, nothing like what you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you avoid certain activities because you're worried you'll get stuck on something or have to do it a certain way?\n\nPatient: Avoid activities? I will literally buy new underwear rather than do laundry if I'm having a busy week. It's terrible, I know. My closet is basically a graveyard of clothes with tags still on them because I keep buying stuff for events instead of just washing what I have. I bought this amazing emerald green silk blouse for a pitch meeting last month, and I haven't worn it since because silk is just impossible to clean. You have to take it to the dry cleaner, and the one on my corner closed down. Now it's a bubble tea place. Which is great, honestly, because their taro milk tea is incredible, but it really doesn't help my laundry situation. The guy who runs it used to work in finance and just quit to open a boba shop. I kind of admire that, you know? Just dropping everything to do something completely different. I think about doing that sometimes, just quitting advertising and opening a dog rescue or something. But then I remember I have rent to pay. So... yeah, I don't avoid things because of having to do them a certain way. Just normal avoiding stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still in situations outside of work?\n\nPatient: Fidgeting? Um... no, not really. I mean, I guess I shift around a bit if I'm sitting for a long time, but nothing crazy. Like when we go to the movies. My boyfriend loves those really long, slow indie films. We went to see this three-hour black-and-white French film last weekend, and the seats at that theater are just awful. They have those old velvet ones that look cool but offer zero back support. I swear my lower back was screaming by the second act. And the guy next to us kept crinkling a candy wrapper. It's like, who eats hard candies at a movie theater anymore? Just get popcorn like a normal person. Popcorn is basically the only reason I even agree to go to the movies anyway. They use real butter at this place, which is amazing, but it gets all over your hands. I always have to bring extra napkins because I hate that greasy feeling on my fingers. It reminds me of this hand cream my mom used to use when I was a kid. It smelled like lavender and old books, and she would always try to put it on me in the winter when my hands got dry. I hated it. But anyway, no, I don't think I'm unusually restless or anything. Just normal fidgeting when I'm uncomfortable.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty sustaining focus on tasks or activities outside of work?\n\nPatient: Focus on things outside of work? I always start out super focused, you know, pen in hand, ready to take notes. They charge like four dollars a can for it, too. It's wild what people will pay for if you put it in a sleek pastel can and tell them it'll reduce their stress. Mind wandering? Um, I guess? I don't know if it's wandering to other things, exactly. It's more like... I'm just not there anymore. Like, I'll be looking at a spreadsheet or someone talking, and then suddenly I'm just zoned out. Like when my sister calls me. She lives in Portland now, and she will talk for literally an hour about her garden. She's obsessed with these heritage tomatoes she's growing. They're all these weird colors, like purple and striped green. She sent me a picture of one that looked exactly like a tiny pumpkin. It was cute, I guess, but I just do not have a green thumb. I tried to keep a succulent alive on my desk at work, and it died within a month. A succulent! They're supposed to be unkillable. Ben told me I probably overwatered it, which is ironic because I always forget to drink water myself. I bought one of those giant water bottles with the time markers on the side to remind me, but it's so heavy when it's full that I hate carrying it on the subway. The subway has been awful lately, by the way. The air conditioning in my car was broken yesterday, and it was just... stifling. But yeah, to answer your question, I'm not really sure if my mind wanders to specific things. I just kind of blank out.

I was actually thinking about pitching a digital out-of-home campaign to one of my clients instead, it's way cleaner. But, um... wait. What did you ask me? Something about checking things? Yeah, no, I don't really do anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and are difficult to stop?\n\nPatient: Repeating thoughts? Um... like, getting stuck on something? No, I don't really get that. I mean, I get songs stuck in my head all the time, obviously. Like right now, I've had that one pop song from the car commercial playing on loop since yesterday morning. It's actually a really smart campaign, they licensed an indie track and now it's everywhere. Just normal stuff like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a strong urge to perform a specific behavior or task over and over?\n\nPatient: Urges to do something over and over? Um... no, not really. Just last week, this one client—he's the VP of marketing for this organic skincare line—he emailed me on a Saturday morning asking for a complete revision of the deck we presented on Thursday. A Saturday! And of course, I didn't see it until Sunday afternoon because I was at my friend Sarah's wedding. It was upstate, in this beautiful converted barn. The lighting was incredible, all these fairy lights strung up across the rafters. But the food was... honestly, kind of a letdown. They had this vegan station that was just sad, wilted kale and some questionable tofu. I mean, I get wanting to be inclusive, but at least make it taste good, you know? My boyfriend ended up ordering a pizza to the hotel room afterward. But yeah, if I hadn't checked my phone when we got back, Monday morning would have been a complete disaster. My closet is basically a graveyard of clothes with tags still on them because I keep buying stuff for events instead of just washing what I have. You have to take it to the dry cleaner, and the one on my corner closed down. Now it's a bubble tea place. Just normal avoiding stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still in situations outside of work?\n\nPatient: Fidgeting? Um... no, not really. I mean, I guess I shift around a bit if I'm sitting for a long time, but nothing crazy. Like when we go to the movies. My boyfriend loves those really long, slow indie films. We went to see this three-hour black-and-white French film last weekend, and the seats at that theater are just awful. They have those old velvet ones that look cool but offer zero back support. I swear my lower back was screaming by the second act. And the guy next to us kept crinkling a candy wrapper. It's like, who eats hard candies at a movie theater anymore? It reminds me of this hand cream my mom used to use when I was a kid. It smelled like lavender and old books, and she would always try to put it on me in the winter when my hands got dry. I hated it. It's so frustrating. It happened just yesterday when we were reviewing the Q3 projections for this new beverage client. They're doing this line of sparkling waters infused with adaptogens. Have you tried those? They're everywhere now. Ashwagandha, maca root, all that stuff. I tried the grapefruit rosemary one, and honestly, it just tasted like dirt. Fancy, carbonated dirt. I mean, my stress would be reduced if I didn't have to sit through a two-hour meeting about carbonated dirt, but here we are. My coworker, Ben, he drinks like three of them a day. I don't know how his stomach handles it. Anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the focus thing. Yeah, I just drift off and totally lose my place.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wanders to other things when you're having trouble focusing?\n\nPatient: Mind wandering? Um, I guess? Like when my sister calls me. She lives in Portland now, and she will talk for literally an hour about her garden. She's obsessed with these heritage tomatoes she's growing. They're all these weird colors, like purple and striped green. She sent me a picture of one that looked exactly like a tiny pumpkin. It was cute, I guess, but I just do not have a green thumb. I tried to keep a succulent alive on my desk at work, and it died within a month. A succulent! They're supposed to be unkillable. Ben told me I probably overwatered it, which is ironic because I always forget to drink water myself. I bought one of those giant water bottles with the time markers on the side to remind me, but it's so heavy when it's full that I hate carrying it on the subway. The subway has been awful lately, by the way. The air conditioning in my car was broken yesterday, and it was just... stifling. I just kind of blank out.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Distracted by things around me? Um, no, not really. I don't think so. I mean, I work in an open-plan office—well, hybrid now, we're only in on Tuesdays and Thursdays. But they just remodeled our whole floor to look like one of those trendy tech startups, you know? With the exposed brick and the hanging plants everywhere. They even put up this huge neon sign right by the breakroom that says "Hustle Harder" in this blinding pink color. It is so obnoxious. 

My friend Sarah actually got a custom neon sign for her wedding last month. It had their new last name on it, which was cute. The wedding was up in the Catskills, absolutely gorgeous venue. It was this restored old barn. But the food was the best part, they had this massive DIY taco bar setup instead of a stuffy sit-down dinner. I love a good taco. My boyfriend and I actually went to this new Mexican place down the street from my apartment last night, and honestly, the guacamole was just... okay. Like, it desperately needed more lime. I feel like restaurants always skimp on the lime. I always have to ask the waiter for extra wedges. 

But anyway, wait, what were you asking? Oh, distractions. No, I don't really pay attention to what's going on around me when I'm trying to get stuff done. I don't really notice that kind of thing.

Overwhelmed by worry? Um, not really. I mean, I have normal stress, like everyone else. Work can be a lot sometimes, especially at the end of the quarter when we're trying to hit our quotas. But I don't sit around worrying for no reason. 

Actually, speaking of quotas, my manager just changed our entire bonus structure last week without warning. It's so frustrating. Um... not really? I mean, I don't think so. I actually really like my weekends. Oh, except for Sundays, sometimes I get that weird Sunday evening feeling, you know? Like, the weekend is over and you have to go back to the grind. My mom used to call it the \"Sunday scaries.\" She would always make a big roast dinner on Sundays to try and make it feel better, with those little roasted potatoes that get crispy on the outside? I tried to make them last week, but I think my oven runs hot because they got completely charred on the bottom. It’s a gas oven, which I thought I wanted, but honestly, it’s been a nightmare to clean. I spent an hour scrubbing the grates with baking soda. But anyway... no, I wouldn't say I feel anxious or on edge normally. Just the normal amount, I guess?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions or rituals outside of work, like checking things or arranging items in a specific way?\n\nPatient: Rituals? Like checking things? Um... no, I don't think so. I don't really have any rituals. Unless you count my morning coffee, which is honestly the only way I survive the commute. I have to get it from this specific little cart near my apartment because the big chain places always burn the espresso. The guy who runs the cart, Hector, he knows my order by heart now. It's a lifesaver because the L train has been completely unpredictable lately. Last Tuesday, I was stuck underground for forty-five minutes with no cell service. Just staring at this ad for a dermatology clinic that was peeling off the wall. It's wild how much money they spend on subway ads when half of them are just vandalized within a week anyway. I was actually thinking about pitching a digital out-of-home campaign to one of my clients instead, it's way cleaner. But, um... wait. What did you ask me? Something about checking things? Yeah, no, I don't really do anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and are difficult to stop?\n\nPatient: Repeating thoughts? Um... like, getting stuck on something? No, I don't really get that. I mean, I get songs stuck in my head all the time, obviously. Like right now, I've had that one pop song from the car commercial playing on loop since yesterday morning. It's actually a really smart campaign, they licensed an indie track and now it's everywhere. We were talking about doing something similar for this boutique fitness brand I'm working with, but their budget is just ridiculously tight. They want champagne results on a tap water budget, you know? And the client lead keeps emailing me at like, 10 PM with these \"urgent\" ideas that are basically just things I already pitched them three weeks ago. I finally had to put my phone on Do Not Disturb after 9, which my boyfriend was thrilled about because my phone screen keeps waking him up. He's such a light sleeper. But anyway... no, I don't have any, like, repeating thoughts that I can't stop. Just normal stuff like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a strong urge to perform a specific behavior or task over and over?\n\nPatient: Urges to do something over and over? Um... no, not really. I mean, I don't feel like I *have* to do anything like that. Well, I do constantly check my phone, but everyone does that, right? Especially in my job. If you miss an email from a client, it's like the end of the world. Just last week, this one client—he's the VP of marketing for this organic skincare line—he emailed me on a Saturday morning asking for a complete revision of the deck we presented on Thursday. A Saturday! And of course, I didn't see it until Sunday afternoon because I was at my friend Sarah's wedding. It was upstate, in this beautiful converted barn. The lighting was incredible, all these fairy lights strung up across the rafters. But the food was... honestly, kind of a letdown. They had this vegan station that was just sad, wilted kale and some questionable tofu. I mean, I get wanting to be inclusive, but at least make it taste good, you know? My boyfriend ended up ordering a pizza to the hotel room afterward. But yeah, if I hadn't checked my phone when we got back, Monday morning would have been a complete disaster. So I guess I check my email a lot, but it's not like a weird urge, it's just work. So no, nothing like what you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you avoid certain activities because you're worried you'll get stuck on something or have to do it a certain way?\n\nPatient: Avoid activities? Like, because I'm worried about getting stuck on them? Um... no, I don't think I do that. I mean, I avoid things all the time, but mostly just because I don't want to do them. Like laundry. I will literally buy new underwear rather than do laundry if I'm having a busy week. It's terrible, I know. My closet is basically a graveyard of clothes with tags still on them because I keep buying stuff for events instead of just washing what I have. I bought this amazing emerald green silk blouse for a pitch meeting last month, and I haven't worn it since because silk is just impossible to clean. You have to take it to the dry cleaner, and the one on my corner closed down. Now it's a bubble tea place. Which is great, honestly, because their taro milk tea is incredible, but it really doesn't help my laundry situation. The guy who runs it used to work in finance and just quit to open a boba shop. I kind of admire that, you know? Just dropping everything to do something completely different. I think about doing that sometimes, just quitting advertising and opening a dog rescue or something. But then I remember I have rent to pay. So... yeah, I don't avoid things because of having to do them a certain way. Just normal avoiding stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still in situations outside of work?\n\nPatient: Fidgeting? Um... no, not really. I mean, I guess I shift around a bit if I'm sitting for a long time, but nothing crazy. Like when we go to the movies. My boyfriend loves those really long, slow indie films. We went to see this three-hour black-and-white French film last weekend, and the seats at that theater are just awful. They have those old velvet ones that look cool but offer zero back support. I swear my lower back was screaming by the second act. And the guy next to us kept crinkling a candy wrapper. It's like, who eats hard candies at a movie theater anymore? Just get popcorn like a normal person. Popcorn is basically the only reason I even agree to go to the movies anyway. They use real butter at this place, which is amazing, but it gets all over your hands. I always have to bring extra napkins because I hate that greasy feeling on my fingers. It reminds me of this hand cream my mom used to use when I was a kid. It smelled like lavender and old books, and she would always try to put it on me in the winter when my hands got dry. I hated it. But anyway, no, I don't think I'm unusually restless or anything. Just normal fidgeting when I'm uncomfortable.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty sustaining focus on tasks or activities outside of work?\n\nPatient: Focus on things outside of work? Wait, outside of work? Actually, it happens at work too. Like, a lot. Especially in those longer strategy meetings or when I have to read through a massive client brief. I always start out super focused, you know, pen in hand, ready to take notes. But then after a few minutes, I'll realize I've just been staring at the exact same slide or the same paragraph for who knows how long, and I have absolutely no idea what the last point was. It's so frustrating. It happened just yesterday when we were reviewing the Q3 projections for this new beverage client. They're doing this line of sparkling waters infused with adaptogens. Have you tried those? They're everywhere now. Ashwagandha, maca root, all that stuff. I tried the grapefruit rosemary one, and honestly, it just tasted like dirt. Fancy, carbonated dirt. They charge like four dollars a can for it, too. It's wild what people will pay for if you put it in a sleek pastel can and tell them it'll reduce their stress. I mean, my stress would be reduced if I didn't have to sit through a two-hour meeting about carbonated dirt, but here we are. My coworker, Ben, he drinks like three of them a day. I don't know how his stomach handles it. Anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the focus thing. Yeah, I just drift off and totally lose my place.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wanders to other things when you're having trouble focusing?\n\nPatient: Mind wandering? Um, I guess? They even put up this huge neon sign right by the breakroom that says \"Hustle Harder\" in this blinding pink color. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Anxious or uneasy about getting up? No, I don't feel anxious about it. Honestly, it's actually what made me decide to come see you. I just keep getting up during those long sales strategy meetings. Like, I know I'm supposed to stay seated, but I just can't. I'll get up to refill my water bottle, or just pace over by the printer. 

Our new printer is so annoying, by the way. It's one of those massive industrial ones that's supposed to do everything—staple, fold, collate—but it jams constantly. Last week, I was trying to print out this pitch deck for a huge client, and it just chewed up like fifty pages of premium glossy paper. The IT guy, Kevin, had to come fix it, and he spent like twenty minutes explaining the internal mechanisms of the paper tray to me. Kevin is really nice, but he talks so slowly. He brought in these homemade brownies once, though, and they were incredible. He said his secret is adding a shot of espresso to the batter, which makes sense because chocolate and coffee are such a good combination. I tried making espresso martinis at home for my boyfriend's birthday last month, but I didn't have a real cocktail shaker, so I used a protein shaker bottle, and it just wasn't the same. The foam didn't get frothy enough. 

But yeah, wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the meetings. Yeah, I just keep getting up when I'm supposed to be sitting down. It's not that I feel uneasy doing it, I just kind of do it.

They use real butter at this place, which is amazing, but it gets all over your hands. I always have to bring extra napkins because I hate that greasy feeling on my fingers. It reminds me of this hand cream my mom used to use when I was a kid. It smelled like lavender and old books, and she would always try to put it on me in the winter when my hands got dry. I hated it. But anyway, no, I don't think I'm unusually restless or anything. Just normal fidgeting when I'm uncomfortable.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty sustaining focus on tasks or activities outside of work?\n\nPatient: Focus on things outside of work? Wait, outside of work? Actually, it happens at work too. Like, a lot. Especially in those longer strategy meetings or when I have to read through a massive client brief. I always start out super focused, you know, pen in hand, ready to take notes. But then after a few minutes, I'll realize I've just been staring at the exact same slide or the same paragraph for who knows how long, and I have absolutely no idea what the last point was. It's so frustrating. It happened just yesterday when we were reviewing the Q3 projections for this new beverage client. They're doing this line of sparkling waters infused with adaptogens. Have you tried those? They're everywhere now. Ashwagandha, maca root, all that stuff. I tried the grapefruit rosemary one, and honestly, it just tasted like dirt. Fancy, carbonated dirt. They charge like four dollars a can for it, too. It's wild what people will pay for if you put it in a sleek pastel can and tell them it'll reduce their stress. I mean, my stress would be reduced if I didn't have to sit through a two-hour meeting about carbonated dirt, but here we are. My coworker, Ben, he drinks like three of them a day. I don't know how his stomach handles it. Anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the focus thing. Yeah, I just drift off and totally lose my place.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wanders to other things when you're having trouble focusing?\n\nPatient: Mind wandering? Um, I guess? I don't know if it's wandering to other things, exactly. It's more like... I'm just not there anymore. Like, I'll be looking at a spreadsheet or someone talking, and then suddenly I'm just zoned out. Like when my sister calls me. She lives in Portland now, and she will talk for literally an hour about her garden. She's obsessed with these heritage tomatoes she's growing. They're all these weird colors, like purple and striped green. She sent me a picture of one that looked exactly like a tiny pumpkin. It was cute, I guess, but I just do not have a green thumb. I tried to keep a succulent alive on my desk at work, and it died within a month. A succulent! They're supposed to be unkillable. Ben told me I probably overwatered it, which is ironic because I always forget to drink water myself. I bought one of those giant water bottles with the time markers on the side to remind me, but it's so heavy when it's full that I hate carrying it on the subway. The subway has been awful lately, by the way. The air conditioning in my car was broken yesterday, and it was just... stifling. But yeah, to answer your question, I'm not really sure if my mind wanders to specific things. I just kind of blank out.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're often distracted by things around you when you're trying to focus?\n\nPatient: Distracted by things around me? Um, no, not really. I don't think so. I mean, I work in an open-plan office—well, hybrid now, we're only in on Tuesdays and Thursdays. But they just remodeled our whole floor to look like one of those trendy tech startups, you know? With the exposed brick and the hanging plants everywhere. They even put up this huge neon sign right by the breakroom that says \"Hustle Harder\" in this blinding pink color. It is so obnoxious. \n\nMy friend Sarah actually got a custom neon sign for her wedding last month. It had their new last name on it, which was cute. The wedding was up in the Catskills, absolutely gorgeous venue. It was this restored old barn. Um, no, not really. I mean, I love talking to people. It's basically my whole job in sales, right? You have to be able to talk to anyone. Like, I met this one client last week who was just... so intense. He was wearing this incredibly sharp suit, like custom-tailored, maybe Italian? You could just tell it was expensive. And he had this vintage Rolex on, which I only noticed because my dad collects watches. He's been trying to find this specific Omega Speedmaster from the '70s for years. He's been really into making his own kombucha lately, which smells terrible, honestly. Anxious? No, no, I just said that, I don't feel anxious. It's just... I can't sit still. That's honestly what made me come in to see you. I keep getting up during these long sales strategy meetings when I know I’m supposed to stay seated. Even if it’s just to refill my water or pace by the printer, I just have to move. 

Actually, I was pacing by the printer yesterday and I noticed they finally put up that new bulletin board HR was talking about. It's cork, but they painted it this weird seafoam green color that completely clashes with the beige walls. Our office manager, Linda, picked it out. She has the most bizarre taste in decor. I've always wanted to go to Costa Rica. They have those incredible sloth sanctuaries, and I just love sloths. They're so weird and slow, which is kind of the opposite of me, honestly. I tried to convince my boyfriend to go there for our anniversary, but he wanted to go skiing in Colorado instead. I am terrible at skiing. Last time I went, I couldn't figure out how to stop and ended up crashing straight into one of those orange mesh fences. It took them like ten minutes to untangle me, and my ski poles were bent completely out of shape. 

Wait, sorry, what was the question? Oh, right, the meetings. Yeah, it's not anxiety, I just really can't stay in my seat when I'm supposed to.

But anyway, yeah, even if I don't actually need to print anything at all, I'll just find myself walking over there or getting more water when everyone else is just sitting down listening to the Q3 projections.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling restless or needing to get up and move around in other situations besides these work meetings?\n\nPatient: Other situations... um, I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed it as much outside of work? Like, I went to the movies this past weekend with my boyfriend to see that new sci-fi movie—he's absolutely obsessed with the director, but honestly, I think all his movies are way too long. It was like three hours! And we got the giant popcorn because they give you a free refill, but they put way too much of that fake butter on it, so I was incredibly thirsty the whole time. We actually had to stop for iced teas on the drive back. But anyway... wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, getting up and moving. Yeah, I don't know, I guess I really only notice it being an issue during those long office pitches.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Anxious or on edge? Um... not really? I mean, I don't think so. I actually really like my weekends. Oh, except for Sundays, sometimes I get that weird Sunday evening feeling, you know? Like, the weekend is over and you have to go back to the grind. My mom used to call it the \"Sunday scaries.\" She would always make a big roast dinner on Sundays to try and make it feel better, with those little roasted potatoes that get crispy on the outside? I tried to make them last week, but I think my oven runs hot because they got completely charred on the bottom. It’s a gas oven, which I thought I wanted, but honestly, it’s been a nightmare to clean. I spent an hour scrubbing the grates with baking soda. But anyway... no, I wouldn't say I feel anxious or on edge normally. Just the normal amount, I guess?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions or rituals outside of work, like checking things or arranging items in a specific way?\n\nPatient: Rituals? Like checking things? Um... no, I don't think so. I don't really have any rituals. Unless you count my morning coffee, which is honestly the only way I survive the commute. I have to get it from this specific little cart near my apartment because the big chain places always burn the espresso. The guy who runs the cart, Hector, he knows my order by heart now. It's a lifesaver because the L train has been completely unpredictable lately. Last Tuesday, I was stuck underground for forty-five minutes with no cell service. Just staring at this ad for a dermatology clinic that was peeling off the wall. It's wild how much money they spend on subway ads when half of them are just vandalized within a week anyway. I was actually thinking about pitching a digital out-of-home campaign to one of my clients instead, it's way cleaner. But, um... wait. What did you ask me? Something about checking things? Yeah, no, I don't really do anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and are difficult to stop?\n\nPatient: Repeating thoughts? Um... like, getting stuck on something? No, I don't really get that. I mean, I get songs stuck in my head all the time, obviously. Like right now, I've had that one pop song from the car commercial playing on loop since yesterday morning. It's actually a really smart campaign, they licensed an indie track and now it's everywhere. We were talking about doing something similar for this boutique fitness brand I'm working with, but their budget is just ridiculously tight. They want champagne results on a tap water budget, you know? And the client lead keeps emailing me at like, 10 PM with these \"urgent\" ideas that are basically just things I already pitched them three weeks ago. I finally had to put my phone on Do Not Disturb after 9, which my boyfriend was thrilled about because my phone screen keeps waking him up. He's such a light sleeper. But anyway... no, I don't have any, like, repeating thoughts that I can't stop. Just normal stuff like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a strong urge to perform a specific behavior or task over and over?\n\nPatient: Urges to do something over and over? Um... no, not really. I mean, I don't feel like I *have* to do anything like that. Well, I do constantly check my phone, but everyone does that, right? Especially in my job. If you miss an email from a client, it's like the end of the world. Just last week, this one client—he's the VP of marketing for this organic skincare line—he emailed me on a Saturday morning asking for a complete revision of the deck we presented on Thursday. A Saturday! And of course, I didn't see it until Sunday afternoon because I was at my friend Sarah's wedding. It was upstate, in this beautiful converted barn. The lighting was incredible, all these fairy lights strung up across the rafters. But the food was... honestly, kind of a letdown. They had this vegan station that was just sad, wilted kale and some questionable tofu. I mean, I get wanting to be inclusive, but at least make it taste good, you know? My boyfriend ended up ordering a pizza to the hotel room afterward. But yeah, if I hadn't checked my phone when we got back, Monday morning would have been a complete disaster. So I guess I check my email a lot, but it's not like a weird urge, it's just work. So no, nothing like what you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you avoid certain activities because you're worried you'll get stuck on something or have to do it a certain way?\n\nPatient: Avoid activities? Like, because I'm worried about getting stuck on them? Um... no, I don't think I do that. I mean, I avoid things all the time, but mostly just because I don't want to do them. Like laundry. I will literally buy new underwear rather than do laundry if I'm having a busy week. It's terrible, I know. My closet is basically a graveyard of clothes with tags still on them because I keep buying stuff for events instead of just washing what I have. I bought this amazing emerald green silk blouse for a pitch meeting last month, and I haven't worn it since because silk is just impossible to clean. You have to take it to the dry cleaner, and the one on my corner closed down. Now it's a bubble tea place. Which is great, honestly, because their taro milk tea is incredible, but it really doesn't help my laundry situation. The guy who runs it used to work in finance and just quit to open a boba shop. I kind of admire that, you know? Just dropping everything to do something completely different. I think about doing that sometimes, just quitting advertising and opening a dog rescue or something. But then I remember I have rent to pay. So... yeah, I don't avoid things because of having to do them a certain way. Just normal avoiding stuff.\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself fidgeting or feeling restless when you're sitting still in situations outside of work?\n\nPatient: Fidgeting? Um... no, not really. I mean, I guess I shift around a bit if I'm sitting for a long time, but nothing crazy. Like when we go to the movies. My boyfriend loves those really long, slow indie films. We went to see this three-hour black-and-white French film last weekend, and the seats at that theater are just awful. They have those old velvet ones that look cool but offer zero back support. I swear my lower back was screaming by the second act. And the guy next to us kept crinkling a candy wrapper. It's like, who eats hard candies at a movie theater anymore? Just get popcorn like a normal person. Popcorn is basically the only reason I even agree to go to the movies anyway. They use real butter at this place, which is amazing, but it gets all over your hands. I always have to bring extra napkins because I hate that greasy feeling on my fingers. It reminds me of this hand cream my mom used to use when I was a kid. It smelled like lavender and old books, and she would always try to put it on me in the winter when my hands got dry. I hated it. But anyway, no, I don't think I'm unusually restless or anything. Just normal fidgeting when I'm uncomfortable.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty sustaining focus on tasks or activities outside of work?\n\nPatient: Focus on things outside of work? Wait, outside of work? Actually, it happens at work too. Like, a lot. Especially in those longer strategy meetings or when I have to read through a massive client brief. I always start out super focused, you know, pen in hand, ready to take notes. But then after a few minutes, I'll realize I've just been staring at the exact same slide or the same paragraph for who knows how long, and I have absolutely no idea what the last point was. It's so frustrating. It happened just yesterday when we were reviewing the Q3 projections for this new beverage client. They're doing this line of sparkling waters infused with adaptogens. Have you tried those? They're everywhere now. Ashwagandha, maca root, all that stuff. I tried the grapefruit rosemary one, and honestly, it just tasted like dirt. Fancy, carbonated dirt. They charge like four dollars a can for it, too. It's wild what people will pay for if you put it in a sleek pastel can and tell them it'll reduce their stress. I mean, my stress would be reduced if I didn't have to sit through a two-hour meeting about carbonated dirt, but here we are. My coworker, Ben, he drinks like three of them a day. I don't know how his stomach handles it. Anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the focus thing. Yeah, I just drift off and totally lose my place.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wanders to other things when you're having trouble focusing?\n\nPatient: Mind wandering? Um, I guess? I don't know if it's wandering to other things, exactly. It's more like... I'm just not there anymore. Like, I'll be looking at a spreadsheet or someone talking, and then suddenly I'm just zoned out. Like when my sister calls me. She lives in Portland now, and she will talk for literally an hour about her garden. She's obsessed with these heritage tomatoes she's growing. They're all these weird colors, like purple and striped green. She sent me a picture of one that looked exactly like a tiny pumpkin. It was cute, I guess, but I just do not have a green thumb. I tried to keep a succulent alive on my desk at work, and it died within a month. A succulent! They're supposed to be unkillable. Ben told me I probably overwatered it, which is ironic because I always forget to drink water myself. I bought one of those giant water bottles with the time markers on the side to remind me, but it's so heavy when it's full that I hate carrying it on the subway. The subway has been awful lately, by the way. The air conditioning in my car was broken yesterday, and it was just... stifling. But yeah, to answer your question, I'm not really sure if my mind wanders to specific things. I just kind of blank out.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're often distracted by things around you when you're trying to focus?\n\nPatient: Distracted by things around me? Um, no, not really. I don't think so. I mean, I work in an open-plan office—well, hybrid now, we're only in on Tuesdays and Thursdays. But they just remodeled our whole floor to look like one of those trendy tech startups, you know? With the exposed brick and the hanging plants everywhere. They even put up this huge neon sign right by the breakroom that says \"Hustle Harder\" in this blinding pink color. It is so obnoxious. \n\nMy friend Sarah actually got a custom neon sign for her wedding last month. It had their new last name on it, which was cute. The wedding was up in the Catskills, absolutely gorgeous venue. It was this restored old barn. But the food was the best part, they had this massive DIY taco bar setup instead of a stuffy sit-down dinner. I love a good taco. My boyfriend and I actually went to this new Mexican place down the street from my apartment last night, and honestly, the guacamole was just... okay. Like, it desperately needed more lime. I feel like restaurants always skimp on the lime. I always have to ask the waiter for extra wedges. \n\nBut anyway, wait, what were you asking? Oh, distractions. No, I don't really pay attention to what's going on around me when I'm trying to get stuff done. I don't really notice that kind of thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or anxious in social situations, like when you're with friends or meeting new people?\n\nPatient: Nervous or anxious? Um, no, not really. I mean, I love talking to people. It's basically my whole job in sales, right? You have to be able to talk to anyone. Like, I met this one client last week who was just... so intense. He was wearing this incredibly sharp suit, like custom-tailored, maybe Italian? You could just tell it was expensive. And he had this vintage Rolex on, which I only noticed because my dad collects watches. He's been trying to find this specific Omega Speedmaster from the '70s for years. He spends hours on eBay and all these weird watch forums just hunting for it. He actually almost got scammed once by a guy in Germany, but luckily he realized the serial number looked sketchy before he wired the money. It's crazy how sophisticated scams are getting now. I got a text the other day pretending to be the post office saying my package was delayed, and it looked so real. I almost clicked the link, but then I remembered I hadn't even ordered anything. Which is rare for me, honestly, because I am definitely guilty of late-night Amazon shopping. Prime shipping is just too convenient. \n\nBut wait, what was the question? Oh, social situations. Yeah, no, I don't really get anxious or anything like that. I'm pretty comfortable around people.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel overwhelmed by worry about everyday things, like work or personal relationships, even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: Overwhelmed by worry? Um, not really. I mean, I have normal stress, like everyone else. Work can be a lot sometimes, especially at the end of the quarter when we're trying to hit our quotas. But I don't sit around worrying for no reason. \n\nActually, speaking of quotas, my manager just changed our entire bonus structure last week without warning. It's so frustrating. He called this mandatory meeting at 8:30 in the morning on a Friday, which is just cruel. Who wants to talk about compensation models before they've even had coffee? I hadn't even gone to my favorite coffee shop yet. It's this little place on the corner that makes their own oat milk in-house. It is so creamy, way better than the stuff you buy at the grocery store. I tried making my own oat milk once during the pandemic, you know, when everyone was baking sourdough and doing weird DIY projects? It was a disaster. It came out all slimy. I watched like three different YouTube tutorials, and they all said to use ice water, which I did, but it still tasted like wet cardboard. My boyfriend laughed so hard when I made him try it. He's been really into making his own kombucha lately, which smells terrible, honestly. It's just sitting in this giant glass jar on our kitchen counter looking like a science experiment. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, worrying. Yeah, no, I don't really feel overwhelmed by worry or anything like that. I just deal with things as they happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or uneasy when you're getting up and moving around during those long sales strategy meetings?\n\nPatient: Anxious or uneasy about getting up? No, I don't feel anxious about it. Honestly, it's actually what made me decide to come see you. I just keep getting up during those long sales strategy meetings. Like, I know I'm supposed to stay seated, but I just can't. I'll get up to refill my water bottle, or just pace over by the printer. \n\nOur new printer is so annoying, by the way. Last week, I was trying to print out this pitch deck for a huge client, and it just chewed up like fifty pages of premium glossy paper. The IT guy, Kevin, had to come fix it, and he spent like twenty minutes explaining the internal mechanisms of the paper tray to me. Kevin is really nice, but he talks so slowly. He brought in these homemade brownies once, though, and they were incredible. He said his secret is adding a shot of espresso to the batter, which makes sense because chocolate and coffee are such a good combination. I tried making espresso martinis at home for my boyfriend's birthday last month, but I didn't have a real cocktail shaker, so I used a protein shaker bottle, and it just wasn't the same. The foam didn't get frothy enough. \n\nBut yeah, wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the meetings. Yeah, I just keep getting up when I'm supposed to be sitting down. It's not that I feel uneasy doing it, I just kind of do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're getting up and moving around during meetings because you're feeling anxious or because you just can't sit still?\n\nPatient: Anxious? No, no, I just said that, I don't feel anxious. It's just... I can't sit still. That's honestly what made me come in to see you. I keep getting up during these long sales strategy meetings when I know I’m supposed to stay seated. Even if it’s just to refill my water or pace by the printer, I just have to move. \n\nActually, I was pacing by the printer yesterday and I noticed they finally put up that new bulletin board HR was talking about. She has this giant collection of porcelain frogs on her desk. Like, dozens of them. She said she started collecting them when she went to Costa Rica for her honeymoon. I've always wanted to go to Costa Rica. They have those incredible sloth sanctuaries, and I just love sloths. They're so weird and slow, which is kind of the opposite of me, honestly. I tried to convince my boyfriend to go there for our anniversary, but he wanted to go skiing in Colorado instead. I am terrible at skiing. Last time I went, I couldn't figure out how to stop and ended up crashing straight into one of those orange mesh fences. It took them like ten minutes to untangle me, and my ski poles were bent completely out of shape. \n\nWait, sorry, what was the question? Oh, right, the meetings. Yeah, it's not anxiety, I just really can't stay in my seat when I'm supposed to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Something inside me? Um, I'm not really sure what you mean by that. I've never really thought about it. I don't think it's the environment, exactly, though our office environment is honestly pretty terrible. They keep the AC cranked so high it feels like an actual meat locker in there. She actually made my sister this huge, gorgeous throw blanket, but then her cat—my sister's cat, Barnaby—completely shredded the fringe on it. 

Barnaby is an absolute menace. He’s an orange tabby, and I swear the jokes about orange cats having zero brain cells are completely true. He got stuck inside a kitchen cabinet for like three hours once because he somehow managed to pull the door shut and engage the child-lock from the inside. My sister had to literally unscrew the hinges to get him out. She was so mad because she had just painted those cabinets this really nice matte navy blue. It took her three weekends to finish the kitchen, and she had to watch all these DIY TikToks to figure out how to sand them down properly without ruining the wood...

Wait, sorry, what did you ask me? Oh, right, if it's the room or something inside me. Yeah, I honestly couldn't tell you. I don't feel like there's some specific thing pushing me, and I don't think it's the room doing it either. I just... I don't know, I just end up walking around.

But anyway... no, I wouldn't say I feel anxious or on edge normally. Just the normal amount, I guess?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions or rituals outside of work, like checking things or arranging items in a specific way?\n\nPatient: Rituals? Like checking things? Um... no, I don't think so. I don't really have any rituals. Unless you count my morning coffee, which is honestly the only way I survive the commute. I have to get it from this specific little cart near my apartment because the big chain places always burn the espresso. The guy who runs the cart, Hector, he knows my order by heart now. It's a lifesaver because the L train has been completely unpredictable lately. Last Tuesday, I was stuck underground for forty-five minutes with no cell service. Just staring at this ad for a dermatology clinic that was peeling off the wall. It's wild how much money they spend on subway ads when half of them are just vandalized within a week anyway. I was actually thinking about pitching a digital out-of-home campaign to one of my clients instead, it's way cleaner. But, um... wait. What did you ask me? Something about checking things? Just normal stuff like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a strong urge to perform a specific behavior or task over and over?\n\nPatient: Urges to do something over and over? Um... no, not really. If you miss an email from a client, it's like the end of the world. A Saturday! And of course, I didn't see it until Sunday afternoon because I was at my friend Sarah's wedding. It was upstate, in this beautiful converted barn. The lighting was incredible, all these fairy lights strung up across the rafters. But the food was... honestly, kind of a letdown. They had this vegan station that was just sad, wilted kale and some questionable tofu. I mean, I get wanting to be inclusive, but at least make it taste good, you know? My boyfriend ended up ordering a pizza to the hotel room afterward. But yeah, if I hadn't checked my phone when we got back, Monday morning would have been a complete disaster. So I guess I check my email a lot, but it's not like a weird urge, it's just work. So no, nothing like what you're asking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you avoid certain activities because you're worried you'll get stuck on something or have to do it a certain way?\n\nPatient: Avoid activities? My closet is basically a graveyard of clothes with tags still on them because I keep buying stuff for events instead of just washing what I have. I swear my lower back was screaming by the second act. And the guy next to us kept crinkling a candy wrapper. It's like, who eats hard candies at a movie theater anymore? It reminds me of this hand cream my mom used to use when I was a kid. It smelled like lavender and old books, and she would always try to put it on me in the winter when my hands got dry. I hated it. Just normal fidgeting when I'm uncomfortable.\n\nDoctor: Do you have difficulty sustaining focus on tasks or activities outside of work?\n\nPatient: Focus on things outside of work? I always start out super focused, you know, pen in hand, ready to take notes. I mean, my stress would be reduced if I didn't have to sit through a two-hour meeting about carbonated dirt, but here we are. My coworker, Ben, he drinks like three of them a day. They even put up this huge neon sign right by the breakroom that says \"Hustle Harder\" in this blinding pink color. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I feel like I'm just repeating myself here, but yeah, it's just something I find myself doing. Like I said, that's literally the main reason I booked this appointment. I just keep getting up during those long sales strategy meetings. I know I'm supposed to stay in my seat, but I just end up wandering off to refill my water bottle or pacing over by the printer. 

Speaking of water bottles, I just bought one of those giant insulated ones that holds like a gallon and has little motivational quotes printed on the side for different times of the day. It's supposed to say things like "Keep going!" at 2 PM, but the font they used is so weird that the "g" looks like a "q," so it looks like it says "Keep quoing!" which isn't even a word. My boyfriend thought it was hilarious. He actually works in graphic design, so bad kerning and weird fonts drive him absolutely crazy. He spent like an hour last night redesigning a menu for this local Thai place we order from just because he couldn't stand how they aligned the prices. Their Pad Thai is amazing, though. They use these really wide, chewy noodles instead of the thin ones, and the peanut sauce has this perfect kick of spice to it. We usually get takeout from there every other Friday... 

Wait, what was I saying? Oh, the meetings. Yeah. I'm not doing it because some voice in my head is telling me to or anything. I just find myself out of my chair when I'm supposed to be sitting down.

But anyway, yeah, even if I don't actually need to print anything at all, I'll just find myself walking over there or getting more water when everyone else is just sitting down listening to the Q3 projections.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling restless or needing to get up and move around in other situations besides these work meetings?\n\nPatient: Other situations... um, I'm not really sure. I haven't really noticed it as much outside of work? Like, I went to the movies this past weekend with my boyfriend to see that new sci-fi movie—he's absolutely obsessed with the director, but honestly, I think all his movies are way too long. It was like three hours! And we got the giant popcorn because they give you a free refill, but they put way too much of that fake butter on it, so I was incredibly thirsty the whole time. We actually had to stop for iced teas on the drive back. But anyway... wait, what were you asking? Oh, right, getting up and moving. Yeah, I don't know, I guess I really only notice it being an issue during those long office pitches.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: Anxious or on edge? Um... not really? I mean, I don't think so. I actually really like my weekends. Oh, except for Sundays, sometimes I get that weird Sunday evening feeling, you know? Like, the weekend is over and you have to go back to the grind. My mom used to call it the \"Sunday scaries.\" She would always make a big roast dinner on Sundays to try and make it feel better, with those little roasted potatoes that get crispy on the outside? I tried to make them last week, but I think my oven runs hot because they got completely charred on the bottom. It’s a gas oven, which I thought I wanted, but honestly, it’s been a nightmare to clean. I spent an hour scrubbing the grates with baking soda. But anyway... no, I wouldn't say I feel anxious or on edge normally. Just the normal amount, I guess?\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeating certain actions or rituals outside of work, like checking things or arranging items in a specific way?\n\nPatient: Rituals? Like checking things? Um... no, I don't think so. I don't really have any rituals. Unless you count my morning coffee, which is honestly the only way I survive the commute. I have to get it from this specific little cart near my apartment because the big chain places always burn the espresso. The guy who runs the cart, Hector, he knows my order by heart now. It's a lifesaver because the L train has been completely unpredictable lately. Last Tuesday, I was stuck underground for forty-five minutes with no cell service. Just staring at this ad for a dermatology clinic that was peeling off the wall. It's wild how much money they spend on subway ads when half of them are just vandalized within a week anyway. I was actually thinking about pitching a digital out-of-home campaign to one of my clients instead, it's way cleaner. But, um... wait. What did you ask me? Something about checking things? Yeah, no, I don't really do anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have thoughts that keep repeating in your mind and are difficult to stop?\n\nPatient: Repeating thoughts? Just normal stuff like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a strong urge to perform a specific behavior or task over and over?\n\nPatient: Urges to do something over and over? Um... no, not really. If you miss an email from a client, it's like the end of the world. Just last week, this one client—he's the VP of marketing for this organic skincare line—he emailed me on a Saturday morning asking for a complete revision of the deck we presented on Thursday. A Saturday! And of course, I didn't see it until Sunday afternoon because I was at my friend Sarah's wedding. It was upstate, in this beautiful converted barn. The lighting was incredible, all these fairy lights strung up across the rafters. But the food was... honestly, kind of a letdown. They had this vegan station that was just sad, wilted kale and some questionable tofu. I mean, I get wanting to be inclusive, but at least make it taste good, you know? My boyfriend ended up ordering a pizza to the hotel room afterward. But yeah, if I hadn't checked my phone when we got back, Monday morning would have been a complete disaster. Wait, outside of work? Actually, it happens at work too. Like, a lot. Especially in those longer strategy meetings or when I have to read through a massive client brief. I always start out super focused, you know, pen in hand, ready to take notes. But then after a few minutes, I'll realize I've just been staring at the exact same slide or the same paragraph for who knows how long, and I have absolutely no idea what the last point was. It's so frustrating. It happened just yesterday when we were reviewing the Q3 projections for this new beverage client. They're doing this line of sparkling waters infused with adaptogens. Have you tried those? They're everywhere now. Ashwagandha, maca root, all that stuff. I tried the grapefruit rosemary one, and honestly, it just tasted like dirt. Fancy, carbonated dirt. They charge like four dollars a can for it, too. It's wild what people will pay for if you put it in a sleek pastel can and tell them it'll reduce their stress. I mean, my stress would be reduced if I didn't have to sit through a two-hour meeting about carbonated dirt, but here we are. My coworker, Ben, he drinks like three of them a day. I don't know how his stomach handles it. Anyway, what was I saying? Oh, right, the focus thing. Yeah, I just drift off and totally lose my place.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your mind wanders to other things when you're having trouble focusing?\n\nPatient: Mind wandering? Um, I guess? I don't know if it's wandering to other things, exactly. It's more like... I'm just not there anymore. Like, I'll be looking at a spreadsheet or someone talking, and then suddenly I'm just zoned out. Like when my sister calls me. She lives in Portland now, and she will talk for literally an hour about her garden. She's obsessed with these heritage tomatoes she's growing. They're all these weird colors, like purple and striped green. She sent me a picture of one that looked exactly like a tiny pumpkin. It was cute, I guess, but I just do not have a green thumb. I tried to keep a succulent alive on my desk at work, and it died within a month. A succulent! They're supposed to be unkillable. Ben told me I probably overwatered it, which is ironic because I always forget to drink water myself. I bought one of those giant water bottles with the time markers on the side to remind me, but it's so heavy when it's full that I hate carrying it on the subway. The subway has been awful lately, by the way. The air conditioning in my car was broken yesterday, and it was just... stifling. But yeah, to answer your question, I'm not really sure if my mind wanders to specific things. I just kind of blank out.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're often distracted by things around you when you're trying to focus?\n\nPatient: Distracted by things around me? Um, no, not really. I don't think so. I mean, I work in an open-plan office—well, hybrid now, we're only in on Tuesdays and Thursdays. But they just remodeled our whole floor to look like one of those trendy tech startups, you know? With the exposed brick and the hanging plants everywhere. They even put up this huge neon sign right by the breakroom that says \"Hustle Harder\" in this blinding pink color. It is so obnoxious. \n\nMy friend Sarah actually got a custom neon sign for her wedding last month. It had their new last name on it, which was cute. The wedding was up in the Catskills, absolutely gorgeous venue. It was this restored old barn. But the food was the best part, they had this massive DIY taco bar setup instead of a stuffy sit-down dinner. I love a good taco. My boyfriend and I actually went to this new Mexican place down the street from my apartment last night, and honestly, the guacamole was just... okay. Like, it desperately needed more lime. I feel like restaurants always skimp on the lime. I always have to ask the waiter for extra wedges. \n\nBut anyway, wait, what were you asking? Oh, distractions. No, I don't really pay attention to what's going on around me when I'm trying to get stuff done. I don't really notice that kind of thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or anxious in social situations, like when you're with friends or meeting new people?\n\nPatient: Nervous or anxious? Um, no, not really. I mean, I love talking to people. It's basically my whole job in sales, right? You have to be able to talk to anyone. Like, I met this one client last week who was just... so intense. He was wearing this incredibly sharp suit, like custom-tailored, maybe Italian? You could just tell it was expensive. And he had this vintage Rolex on, which I only noticed because my dad collects watches. He's been trying to find this specific Omega Speedmaster from the '70s for years. He spends hours on eBay and all these weird watch forums just hunting for it. He actually almost got scammed once by a guy in Germany, but luckily he realized the serial number looked sketchy before he wired the money. It's crazy how sophisticated scams are getting now. I got a text the other day pretending to be the post office saying my package was delayed, and it looked so real. I almost clicked the link, but then I remembered I hadn't even ordered anything. Which is rare for me, honestly, because I am definitely guilty of late-night Amazon shopping. Prime shipping is just too convenient. \n\nBut wait, what was the question? Oh, social situations. Yeah, no, I don't really get anxious or anything like that. I'm pretty comfortable around people.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel overwhelmed by worry about everyday things, like work or personal relationships, even when there's no obvious reason to worry?\n\nPatient: Overwhelmed by worry? Um, not really. I mean, I have normal stress, like everyone else. Work can be a lot sometimes, especially at the end of the quarter when we're trying to hit our quotas. But I don't sit around worrying for no reason. \n\nActually, speaking of quotas, my manager just changed our entire bonus structure last week without warning. It's so frustrating. He called this mandatory meeting at 8:30 in the morning on a Friday, which is just cruel. Who wants to talk about compensation models before they've even had coffee? I hadn't even gone to my favorite coffee shop yet. It's this little place on the corner that makes their own oat milk in-house. It is so creamy, way better than the stuff you buy at the grocery store. I tried making my own oat milk once during the pandemic, you know, when everyone was baking sourdough and doing weird DIY projects? It was a disaster. It came out all slimy. I watched like three different YouTube tutorials, and they all said to use ice water, which I did, but it still tasted like wet cardboard. My boyfriend laughed so hard when I made him try it. He's been really into making his own kombucha lately, which smells terrible, honestly. It's just sitting in this giant glass jar on our kitchen counter looking like a science experiment. \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, worrying. Yeah, no, I don't really feel overwhelmed by worry or anything like that. I just deal with things as they happen.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel anxious or uneasy when you're getting up and moving around during those long sales strategy meetings?\n\nPatient: Anxious or uneasy about getting up? No, I don't feel anxious about it. Honestly, it's actually what made me decide to come see you. I just keep getting up during those long sales strategy meetings. Like, I know I'm supposed to stay seated, but I just can't. I'll get up to refill my water bottle, or just pace over by the printer. \n\nOur new printer is so annoying, by the way. It's one of those massive industrial ones that's supposed to do everything—staple, fold, collate—but it jams constantly. Last week, I was trying to print out this pitch deck for a huge client, and it just chewed up like fifty pages of premium glossy paper. The IT guy, Kevin, had to come fix it, and he spent like twenty minutes explaining the internal mechanisms of the paper tray to me. Kevin is really nice, but he talks so slowly. He brought in these homemade brownies once, though, and they were incredible. He said his secret is adding a shot of espresso to the batter, which makes sense because chocolate and coffee are such a good combination. I tried making espresso martinis at home for my boyfriend's birthday last month, but I didn't have a real cocktail shaker, so I used a protein shaker bottle, and it just wasn't the same. The foam didn't get frothy enough. \n\nBut yeah, wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the meetings. Yeah, I just keep getting up when I'm supposed to be sitting down. I keep getting up during these long sales strategy meetings when I know I’m supposed to stay seated. Even if it’s just to refill my water or pace by the printer, I just have to move. \n\nActually, I was pacing by the printer yesterday and I noticed they finally put up that new bulletin board HR was talking about. She has this giant collection of porcelain frogs on her desk. Like, dozens of them. She said she started collecting them when she went to Costa Rica for her honeymoon. I've always wanted to go to Costa Rica. They have those incredible sloth sanctuaries, and I just love sloths. They're so weird and slow, which is kind of the opposite of me, honestly. I tried to convince my boyfriend to go there for our anniversary, but he wanted to go skiing in Colorado instead. I am terrible at skiing. Last time I went, I couldn't figure out how to stop and ended up crashing straight into one of those orange mesh fences. It took them like ten minutes to untangle me, and my ski poles were bent completely out of shape. \n\nWait, sorry, what was the question? Oh, right, the meetings. Yeah, it's not anxiety, I just really can't stay in my seat when I'm supposed to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there's something inside you that's making you get up and move around, or is it more about the situation or environment around you?\n\nPatient: Something inside me? They use these really wide, chewy noodles instead of the thin ones, and the peanut sauce has this perfect kick of spice to it. We usually get takeout from there every other Friday... \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, the meetings. Yeah. I'm not doing it because some voice in my head is telling me to or anything. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.
